> Gatyatthakammani-gāmassa pādena gato, nagarassa pādena gato, appo saggāya gacchati, saggassa gamanena vā, mūlāya paṭikasseyya saṅgho. Dutiyā ca, gāmaṃ pādena gato, nagaraṃ pādena gato, appo saggaṃ gacchati, saggaṃ gamanena vā, mūlaṃ paṭikasseyya saṅgho iccevamādi.

> Āsīsatthe-āyasmato dīghāyuko hotu, bhaddaṃ bhavato hotu, kusalaṃ bhavato hotu, anāmayaṃ bhavato hotu, sukhaṃ bhavato hotu, svāgataṃ bhavato hotu, attho bhavato hotu, hitaṃ bhavato hotu iccevamādi.

> Sammutippayoge– aññatra saṅghasammutiyā bhikkhussa vippavatthuṃ na vaṭṭati, sādhu sammuti me tassa bhagavato dassanāya iccevamādi.

> Bhiyyappayoge – bhiyyoso mattāya[^8] iccevamādi.

> Sattamyatthe – tuyhañcassa āvi karomi, tassa me sakko pāturahosi iccevamādi.

:::tip[Examples]

**Gatyatthakammani** — the object of a verb of going: the destination. `Dutiyā ca`: the second form ② too, which is the ordinary construction.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(gāma) | gāmassa pādena gato | gone to the village on foot |
| 🚻④⨀(nagara) | nagarassa pādena gato | gone to the city on foot |
| 🚹④⨀(sagga) | appo saggāya gacchati | few go to heaven |
| 🚹④⨀(sagga) | saggassa gamanena vā | or: by going to heaven |
| 🚻④⨀(mūla) | mūlāya paṭikasseyya saṅgho | the Saṅgha should send [him] back to the beginning |
| 🚹②⨀(gāma) 🚻②⨀(nagara) | gāmaṃ pādena gato, nagaraṃ pādena gato | gone to the village, to the city, on foot (②) |
| 🚹②⨀(sagga) | appo saggaṃ gacchati, saggaṃ gamanena vā | few go to heaven; or by going to heaven (②) |
| 🚻②⨀(mūla) | mūlaṃ paṭikasseyya saṅgho | the Saṅgha should send [him] back to the beginning (②) |

> `sakuṇo jālamuttova, appo saggāya gacchati` \
> [(18Dh 13.7: Pesakāradhītāvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/13/13.7#187) \
🚹①⨀(sakuṇa) 🚹①⨀(jālamutta) 🔼(iva) 🚹①⨀(appa) 🚹④⨀(sagga) ▶️🤟⨀(gacchati) \
a bird | freed from the net | like | few | to heaven | go

> `parivāsaṃ dadeyya, mūlāya paṭikasseyya, mānattaṃ dadeyya` \
> [(3V 9.5: Pārivāsikādikathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/9/9.5#1806) \
🚹②⨀(parivāsa) ⏯🤟⨀(dadāti) 🚻④⨀(mūla) ⏯🤟⨀(paṭikassati) 🚻②⨀(mānatta) ⏯🤟⨀(dadāti) \
probation | it should grant | to the beginning | it should send back | penance | it should grant

**Āsīsatthe** — in wishing well: the one to whom good is wished.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(āyasmantu) | āyasmato dīghāyuko hotu | may the venerable one be long-lived |
| 🚹④⨀(bhavanta) | bhaddaṃ bhavato hotu, kusalaṃ bhavato hotu, anāmayaṃ bhavato hotu, sukhaṃ bhavato hotu | may there be good fortune, wellbeing, health, happiness for you, sir |
| 🚹④⨀(bhavanta) | svāgataṃ bhavato hotu, attho bhavato hotu, hitaṃ bhavato hotu | welcome to you, sir; may there be gain, benefit for you |

**Sammutippayoge** — with `sammuti` ("consent", "agreement"): the one for whom.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(bhikkhu) | aññatra saṅghasammutiyā bhikkhussa vippavatthuṃ na vaṭṭati | except by the Saṅgha's consent it is not proper for a monk to live apart |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | sādhu sammuti me tassa bhagavato dassanāya | good is the agreement for me to see that Blessed One |

**Bhiyyappayoge** — with `bhiyya` ("more"): the measure exceeded.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺④⨀(mattā) | bhiyyoso mattāya | to a still greater measure, exceedingly |

> `bhiyyoso mattāya` \
> [(1V 1.2.6: Kuṭikārasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.6#1427) \
🔼(bhiyyoso) 🚺④⨀(mattā) \
still more | to a measure

**Sattamyatthe** — in the sense of the seventh: where the locative "in", "before" could stand.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️④⨀(tumha) | tuyhañcassa āvi karomi | and I make it plain to you |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | tassa me sakko pāturahosi | then Sakka appeared to me |

> `te te āvikaromi sakkhipuṭṭho` \
> [(18Sn 1.5: Cundasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Sn/1/1.5#98) \
⚧️②⨂(ta) ⚧️④⨀(tumha) ▶️👆⨀(āvikaroti) 🚹①⨀(sakkhipuṭṭha) \
them | to you | I make plain | asked as a witness

:::

> Atthaggahaṇena bahūsu akkharappayogesu dissati.

> Taṃ yathā? Upamaṃ te[^9] karissāmi, dhammaṃ vo desessāmi.

> Sāratthe ca – desetu bhante bhagavā dhammaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ. Tassa phāsu vihārāya hoti, etassa pahiṇeyya, yathā no bhagavā byākareyya, tathāpi tesaṃ byākarissāma, kappati samaṇānaṃ āyogo, amhākaṃ maṇinā attho, kimattho me buddhena, seyyo me attho, bahūpakārā bhante mahāpajā patigotamī bhagavato, bahūpakārā bhikkhave mātāpitaro puttānaṃ iccevamādi.

> Sesesu akkharappayogesupi aññepi payogā payogavicakkhaṇehi yojetabbā.

By the word `attha` (sense) [in `sattamyatthesu`], [the fourth form] is seen in many constructions of words (`akkhara`). And in the sense of the essential [word] (`sārattha`) [it is seen too]. In the remaining constructions of words as well, the other usages are to be applied by those skilled in usage.

:::tip[Examples]

The vutti's label `sārattha` is glossed by the Nyāsa as `uttamattha`, "the highest sense", or `cintāpanattha`, "the sense that prompts thought" (`sārattho nāma uttamattho, cintāpanattho vā`; so too the Saddanīti)[^29]; what follows it are the everyday fourth forms of asking a teaching for someone, sending to, being allowable for, needing, and being of service to, which the Rūpasiddhi (303) gathers under the `ca` of the sutta (`pahiṇati kappati pahoti upamā añjalikaraṇa phāsu attha seyya`).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️④⨀(tumha) | upamaṃ te karissāmi | I shall make a simile for you |
| ⚧️④⨂(tumha) | dhammaṃ vo desessāmi | I shall teach you the Dhamma |
| 🚹④⨂(bhikkhu) | desetu bhante bhagavā dhammaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ | let the Blessed One, sir, teach the Dhamma to the monks |
| 🚹④⨀(phāsuvihāra) | tassa phāsuvihārāya hoti | it is for his comfortable living |
| ⚧️④⨀(eta) | etassa pahiṇeyya | he should send to him |
| ⚧️④⨂(amha) | yathā no bhagavā byākareyya | as the Blessed One would explain to us |
| ⚧️④⨂(ta) | tathāpi tesaṃ byākarissāma | even so we shall explain to them |
| 🚹④⨂(samaṇa) | kappati samaṇānaṃ āyogo | the practice is allowable for ascetics |
| ⚧️④⨂(amha) | amhākaṃ maṇinā attho | we have need of the jewel |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | kimattho me buddhena | what need have I of the Buddha? |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | seyyo me attho | the better goal is for me |
| 🚹④⨀(bhagavantu) | bahūpakārā bhante mahāpajāpatigotamī bhagavato | Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī, sir, has been of great service to the Blessed One |
| 🚹④⨂(putta) | bahūpakārā bhikkhave mātāpitaro puttānaṃ | parents, monks, are of great service to their children |

> `tena hi, rājañña, upamaṃ te karissāmi` \
> [(7D 10.2.10: Aggikajaṭilaupamā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/10/10.2/10.2.10#1117) \
🔼(tena) 🔼(hi) 🚹⓪⨀(rājañña) 🚺②⨀(upamā) ⚧️④⨀(tumha) ⏯👆⨀(karoti) \
well then | indeed | chieftain | a simile | for you | I shall make

> `dhammaṃ vo, bhikkhave, desessāmi` \
> [(11M 5.6: Chachakkasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/11M/5/5.6#1192) \
🚹②⨀(dhamma) ⚧️④⨂(tumha) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) ⏯👆⨀(deseti) \
the Dhamma | to you | monks | I shall teach

> `desetu, bhante, bhagavā dhammaṃ` \
> [(3V 1.5: Brahmayācanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.5#29) \
⏯🤟⨀(deseti) 🚹⓪⨀(bhadanta) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) 🚹②⨀(dhamma) \
let teach | venerable sir | the Blessed One | the Dhamma

> `yathā no bhagavā byākareyya` \
> [(9M 2.8: Madhupiṇḍikasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/9M/2/2.8#707) \
🔼(yathā) ⚧️④⨂(amha) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) ⏯🤟⨀(byākaroti) \
as | to us | the Blessed One | would explain

> `na kappati samaṇānaṃ sakyaputtiyānaṃ evarūpaṃ kātuṃ` \
> [(1V 1.2.5: Sañcarittasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.5#1237) \
🔼(na) ▶️🤟⨀(kappati) 🚹④⨂(samaṇa) 🚹④⨂(sakyaputtiya) 🚻②⨀(evarūpa) 🔼(kātuṃ) \
not | it is allowable | for ascetics | for the sons of the Sakyan | such a thing | to do

> `bhikkhussa maṇinā attho` \
> [(1V 1.2.6: Kuṭikārasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.6#1422) \
🚹④⨀(bhikkhu) 🚹③⨀(maṇi) 🚹①⨀(attha) \
the monk | of the jewel | has need

> `bahūpakārā, bhante, mahāpajāpati gotamī bhagavato` \
> [(4V 10.1.1: Mahāpajāpatigotamīvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/10/10.1/10.1.1#1968) \
🚺①⨀(bahūpakāra) 🚹⓪⨀(bhadanta) 🚺①⨀(mahāpajāpati) 🚺①⨀(gotamī) 🚹④⨀(bhagavantu) \
of great service | venerable sir | Mahāpajāpatī | Gotamī | to the Blessed One

> `bahukārā, bhikkhave, mātāpitaro puttānaṃ` \
> [(15A2 1.4.2)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/15A2/1/1.4/1.4.2#72) \
🚹①⨂(bahukāra) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨂(mātāpitu) 🚹④⨂(putta) \
of great service | monks | parents | to their children

The Aṅguttara reads `bahukārā` where the vutti has `bahūpakārā`.

:::

> Caggahaṇaṃ vikappanatthavāggahaṇānukaḍḍhanatthaṃ. Ye keci saddā sampadānappayogikā mayā nopadiṭṭhā, tesaṃ gahaṇatthaṃ idha vikappīyati vāsaddo.

> Taṃ yathā? Bhikkhusaṅghassa pabhū ayaṃ bhagavā, desassa pabhū ayaṃ rājā. Khettassa pabhū ayaṃ gahapati, araññassa pabhū ayaṃ luddako iccevamādi. Kvaci dutiyā tatiyā pañcamī chaṭṭhī sattamyatthesu ca.

The word `ca` is there to draw down the word `vā` in its sense of opening the option: whatever words with a `sampadāna` construction I have not shown, the word `vā` is opened here to take them in. And sometimes [the fourth form stands] in the senses of the second, third, fifth, sixth and seventh.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(bhikkhusaṅgha) | bhikkhusaṅghassa pabhū ayaṃ bhagavā | this Blessed One is master over the community of monks |
| 🚹④⨀(desa) | desassa pabhū ayaṃ rājā | this king is master over the country |
| 🚻④⨀(khetta) | khettassa pabhū ayaṃ gahapati | this householder is master over the field |
| 🚻④⨀(arañña) | araññassa pabhū ayaṃ luddako | this hunter is master over the forest |

With `pabhū` ("lord over") the fourth and the sixth form have the same shape, `bhikkhusaṅghassa`; the vutti counts it a `sampadāna`. Its closing `kvaci` is the restrictive marker of the two levels: only in some places does the fourth form stand for another — the `saggāya gacchati` (② sense) and `hiraññasuvaṇṇena` (③ sense) above, and the constructions §306–§315 will list.

:::

[^7]: CST4 prints `akathīta kammaṃ`; the same sentence has `kathitakammaṃ`, and the Rūpasiddhi reads `akathitakammaṃ`.

[^8]: CST4 prints `mattāyaṃ`; the canonical idiom is `bhiyyoso mattāya`, and the rule assigns the fourth form, not the seventh.

[^9]: CST4 prints `Upamaṃ mata karissāmi`; the canonical phrase is `upamaṃ te karissāmi`, and the enclitic `te` (④) is the point, parallel to `vo` in `dhammaṃ vo desessāmi`.

[^27]: U Nandisena (2005) renders `akathitassa` "the person that does not talk", marking the sentence for checking. `akathita` is the past participle "unstated", the word the vutti uses below for the `akathitakamma` (`bhikkhū` in `bhagavā bhikkhū etadavoca`); "one who does not talk" would need an active form, and the examples `ārādhohaṃ rañño`, `kyāhaṃ ayyānaṃ aparajjhāmi` ask after a person the sentence does not otherwise state as its object.

[^28]: The vutti's `yassa kammuno pubbassa yo kattā` has a relative `yassa` without a correlative; the Rūpasiddhi (303) reads `pubbassa kammuno yo kattā, so sampadānasañño hoti` without it, and Nandisena's edition judges the word superfluous (`yassāti padaṃ adhikanti maññe … padarūpasiddhiyampi taṃ natthi`). It is at most a loose relative, "the agent of whatever prior action", and the translation does not build on it.

[^29]: Nandisena's edition gives the Nyāsa's gloss (`sārattho nāma uttamattho, cintāpanattho vā`) with the Saddanīti (p. 126), and records the Sinhalese reading `sādaratthe`, "in the sense of respect", which would suit the deferential `desetu bhante bhagavā dhammaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ`.

<a name="m278"></a>

### §278 (320): Yodhāro tamokāsaṃ (the support of the action: `okāsa`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | yo ādhāro — taṃ (kārakaṃ) | (saññaṃ hoti) | okāsaṃ | | |
|        |        | that which is the support [of the action] — that `kāraka` | (is named) | `okāsa` | | |

The sutta is `yo ādhāro taṃ okāsaṃ`: `yodhāro` elides the `ā` of `ādhāro` after `yo` by [§13 (15): Vā paro asarūpā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m13), and `tamokāsaṃ` is `taṃ okāsaṃ` by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34). `ādhāra` (`ā` + `√dhar`, "what holds up") is the support in which the action of agent and object is placed — the Rūpasiddhi: `kattukammasamavetānaṃ nisajjapacanādikriyānaṃ patiṭṭhānaṭṭhena yo ādhāro`, "the support, in the sense of the footing, of the sitting, cooking and other actions that inhere in agent and object". The vutti supplies `kārakaṃ` and `saññaṃ hoti`. `okāsa`, "open space, room", names the place the action occupies, so the definition is `anvattha`; there is no option marker. The vutti then sorts the support into four kinds: `byāpika` (pervading — the thing held spreads through the whole of what holds it: milk in water, oil in sesame), `opasilesika` (by contact — the king on his couch, the Saṅgha on its seat), `vesayika` (by domain — men move on the earth, winds in the sky, birds in the air) and `sāmīpika` (by proximity — "on the Ganges" for a village on its bank, "at Sāvatthī" for a grove outside it). §279–§283 go on to define `karaṇa`, `kamma`, `kattā`, `hetu` and `sāmī`; the Rūpasiddhi cites this rule at 93 as well as 320.

:::

> Yo ādhāro, taṃ okāsasaññaṃ hoti. Svādhāro catubbidho byāpiko, opasilesiko, vesayiko sāmīpiko cāti.

> Tattha byāpiko tāva–jalesu khīraṃ tiṭṭhati, tilesu telaṃ, ucchūsu raso.

> Opasilesiko–pariyaṅke rājā seti, āsane upaviṭṭho saṅgho.

> Vesayiko–bhūmīsu manussā caranti, antalikkhe vāyū vāyanti. Ākāse sakuṇā pakkhandanti.

> Sāmīpiko–vane hatthino caranti, gaṅgāyaṃ ghoso tiṭṭhati, vaje gāvo duhanti, sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane.

> Okāsamiccanena kvattho? Okāse sattamī.

That which is the support (`ādhāra`) [of the action] is termed `okāsa` (the location). That support is fourfold: pervading (`byāpika`), by contact (`opasilesika`), by domain (`vesayika`) and by proximity (`sāmīpika`).

:::tip[Examples]

In each phrase the word in the seventh form ⑦ is the `okāsa`; the lemma column names it.

**Byāpiko** — pervading: the thing held spreads through the whole of its support.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻⑦⨂(jala) | jalesu khīraṃ tiṭṭhati | milk stands in water |
| 🚻⑦⨂(tila) | tilesu telaṃ | oil in sesame seeds |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ucchu) | ucchūsu raso | juice in sugar canes |

**Opasilesiko** — by contact: the thing rests against its support.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(pariyaṅka) | pariyaṅke rājā seti | the king lies on the couch |
| 🚻⑦⨀(āsana) | āsane upaviṭṭho saṅgho | the Saṅgha seated on the seat |

**Vesayiko** — by domain: the support is the sphere within which the thing moves.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨂(bhūmi) | bhūmīsu manussā caranti | men walk on the earth |
| 🚻⑦⨀(antalikkha) | antalikkhe vāyū vāyanti | winds blow in the sky |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ākāsa) | ākāse sakuṇā pakkhandanti | birds fly in the air |

**Sāmīpiko** — by proximity: the support is named for what lies near it.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻⑦⨀(vana) | vane hatthino caranti | elephants roam in the forest |
| 🚺⑦⨀(gaṅgā) | gaṅgāyaṃ ghoso tiṭṭhati | the cowherds' village stands on the Ganges |
| 🚹⑦⨀(vaja) | vaje gāvo duhanti | they milk the cows in the pen |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sāvatthī) 🚻⑦⨀(jetavana) | sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane | he dwells at Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove |

> `buddho bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme` \
> [(1V 1.2.1: Sukkavissaṭṭhisikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.1#775) \
🚹①⨀(buddha) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) 🚺⑦⨀(sāvatthī) ▶️🤟⨀(viharati) 🚻⑦⨀(jetavana) 🚹⑥⨀(anāthapiṇḍika) 🚹⑦⨀(ārāma) \
the Buddha | the Blessed One | at Sāvatthī | dwells | in Jeta's Grove | of Anāthapiṇḍika | in the park

`sāvatthiyaṃ` takes the `yaṃ` of [§216 (180): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m216), `gaṅgāyaṃ` likewise; `jetavane`, `vane`, `āsane` the `e` of [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m108); `jalesu`, `tilesu` the `e` before `su` of [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m101). The Rūpasiddhi (320) adds that a support is called `okāsa` whether it holds the action in fact or by a figure — `padhānavasena vā parikappitavasena vā`.

:::

:::tip[Purpose]

`Okāsamiccanena kvattho?` — what is the use of the term `okāsa`? `Okāse sattamī`: it is the term through which [§302 (319): Okāse sattamī](#m302) assigns the seventh form ⑦. The Bālāvatāra (¶57) joins the two rules into one.

:::

<a name="m279"></a>

### §279 (292): Yena vā kayirate taṃ karaṇaṃ (that by which [the action] is done: `karaṇa`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     |        | yena kayirate (passati, suṇāti) — taṃ (kārakaṃ) | (saññaṃ hoti) | karaṇaṃ | | |
| or     |        | that by which [something] is done, seen or heard — that `kāraka` | (is named) | `karaṇa` | | |

`yena` is ③⨀ "by which"; `kayirate` is the passive of `karoti`, "is done". The vutti spells out what the `vā` adds: that by which one sees (`passati`) or hears (`suṇāti`) — the eye and the ear — is an instrument too, though nothing is "done" with it; and the Rūpasiddhi (284, 285) says this `vā` carries over into the definition of the `kamma` and serves there for the same "other senses" (`atthantaravikappana`). It is the open `vā` of the definitions explained under [§276 (302): Yassa dātukāmo rocate dhārayate vā taṃ sampadānaṃ](#m276). The vutti supplies `kārakaṃ` and `saññaṃ hoti`. `karaṇa` (`√kara` + `aṇa`, "the doing", "the means of doing") says what it means — the Rūpasiddhi: `karīyate anenāti karaṇaṃ`, "that by which it is done", the most effective (`sādhakatama`) of the agent's aids, whether internal (the hand, the eye, the mind) or external (a sickle, a fire) — so the definition is `anvattha`. The Rūpasiddhi cites the rule at 82 as well as 292.

:::

> Yena vā kayirate, yena vā passati, yena vā suṇāti, taṃ kārakaṃ karaṇasaññaṃ hoti.

> Dattena vīhiṃ[^10] lunāti, vāsiyā kaṭṭhaṃ tacchati, pharasunā rukkhaṃ chindati, kudālena pathaviṃ[^30] khaṇati, satthena kammaṃ karoti. Cakkhunā rūpaṃ passati.

> Karaṇamiccanena kvattho? Karaṇe tatiyā.

That by which [something] is done, that by which [one] sees, or that by which [one] hears — that `kāraka` is termed `karaṇa` (the instrument).

:::tip[Examples]

> `dattena vīhiṃ lunāti` \
🚻③⨀(datta) 🚹②⨀(vīhi) ▶️🤟⨀(lunāti) \
with a sickle | the rice | he reaps

`dattena` (③⨀; the usual spelling is `dātta`) is the `karaṇa`, the instrument; `vīhiṃ` is the `kamma` in ②.

> `vāsiyā kaṭṭhaṃ tacchati` \
🚺③⨀(vāsī) 🚻②⨀(kaṭṭha) ▶️🤟⨀(tacchati) \
with an adze | the wood | he planes

> `araṇisahitaṃ vāsiyā tacchi` \
> [(7D 10.2.10: Aggikajaṭilaupamā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/10/10.2/10.2.10#1118) \
🚻②⨀(araṇisahita) 🚺③⨀(vāsī) ⏮🤟⨀(tacchati) \
the fire-drill and its base | with an adze | he planed

> `pharasunā rukkhaṃ chindati` \
🚹③⨀(pharasu) 🚹②⨀(rukkha) ▶️🤟⨀(chindati) \
with an axe | the tree | he cuts

> `tato sā bhañjatha sākhā, chinnā pharasunā viya` \
> [(22J 16.1.6: Mahākapijātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/16/16.1/16.1.6#3451) \
⚧️⑤⨀(ta + to) 🚺①⨀(ta) ⏮🤟⨀(bhañjati) 🚺①⨀(sākhā) 🚺①⨀(chinna) 🚹③⨀(pharasu) 🔼(viya) \
then | that | broke | branch | cut | with an axe | as if

> `kudālena pathaviṃ khaṇati` \
🚹③⨀(kudāla) 🚺②⨀(pathavī) ▶️🤟⨀(khaṇati) \
with a spade | the earth | he digs

> `satthena kammaṃ karoti` \
🚻③⨀(sattha) 🚻②⨀(kamma) ▶️🤟⨀(karoti) \
with a knife | the work | he does

> `cakkhunā rūpaṃ passati` \
> [(13S4 1.3.4.2: Khaṇasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/13S4/1/1.3/1.3.4/1.3.4.2#777) \
🚻③⨀(cakkhu) 🚻②⨀(rūpa) ▶️🤟⨀(passati) \
with the eye | a form | he sees

`cakkhunā` is the instrument of the `yena vā passati` clause: the eye is not used to "do" anything, and the `vā` brings it in.[^31] `vāsiyā` takes the `yā` of [§112 (183): Pato yā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m112), `pharasunā` and `cakkhunā` the `nā` after a `la` stem.

:::

:::tip[Purpose]

`Karaṇamiccanena kvattho?` — what is the use of the term `karaṇa`? `Karaṇe tatiyā`: it is the term through which [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286) assigns the third form ③. The Bālāvatāra (¶52) joins the two rules.

:::

[^10]: CST4 prints `vihiṃ`; rice is `vīhi`, as the vutti of [§297 (284): Kammatthe dutiyā](#m297) (`vīhayo lunāti`) and the Rūpasiddhi (`dattena vīhayo lunāti`) spell it.

[^30]: Nandisena's edition records the Sinhalese reading `kudālena āvāṭaṃ khaṇati`, "with a spade he digs a pit"; either object suits `khaṇati`, and neither touches the rule.

[^31]: Nandisena's edition closes the list with `sotena saddaṃ suṇāti`, "with the ear he hears a sound", answering the vutti's third clause `yena vā suṇāti`; the CST text stops at `cakkhunā rūpaṃ passati`, and the sentence is quoted under [§280 (285): Yaṃ karoti taṃ kammaṃ](#m280) as a `karaṇa` of this rule.

---

<a name="m280"></a>

### §280 (285): Yaṃ karoti taṃ kammaṃ (what one does: `kamma`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   |        | yaṃ karoti (passati, suṇāti) — taṃ (kārakaṃ) | (saññaṃ hoti) | kammaṃ | | |
| (or)   |        | what [one] does, sees or hears — that `kāraka` | (is named) | `kamma` | | |

`yaṃ` is ②⨀ "what"; the sutta itself has no `vā`, but the vutti reads `yaṃ vā karoti, yaṃ vā passati, yaṃ vā suṇāti`: the `vā` is carried over from [§279 (292): Yena vā kayirate taṃ karaṇaṃ](#m279), as the Rūpasiddhi states in so many words (`‘‘Yena vā kayirate taṃ karaṇa’’nti ito ‘‘vā’’ti vattate`), and again opens the definition to other verbs — what one sees, hears, treads on or swallows, whether wished for or not (the Rūpasiddhi's `anicchitakamma`: `kaṇṭakaṃ maddati`, `visaṃ gilati`) and whether or not it is the object actually named (`akathitakamma`, the `bhikkhū` of `bhagavā bhikkhū etadavoca` in [§277 (303): Silāgha hanu ṭhā sapa dhāra piha kudha duhisso ssūya rādhikkha paccāsuṇa anupatigiṇa pubbakattārocanattha tadattha tumatthālamattha maññānādarappāṇini gatyatthakammaniāsisatthasammutibhiyyasattamyatthesu ca](#m277)). The vutti supplies `kārakaṃ` and `saññaṃ hoti`. `kamma` (`√kara` + `ma`, "what is done") says what it means — `karīyatīti kammaṃ` — so the badge is `anvattha`. The Rūpasiddhi (285) divides the object into what is produced (`nibbattanīya`: a pot), what is altered (`vikaraṇīya`: wood into charcoal) and what is reached (`pāpaṇīya`: a village one enters), and here defines the `kāraka` itself: `kriyānipphattiyā kāraṇaṃ`, "the means of bringing the action about", sixfold. The rule is cited twice in the Rūpasiddhi, at 75 and 285.

:::

> Yaṃ vā karoti, yaṃ vā passati, yaṃ vā suṇāti, taṃ kārakaṃ kammasaññaṃ hoti.

> Chattaṃ karoti, rathaṃ karoti, rūpaṃ passati, saddaṃ suṇāti, kaṇṭakaṃ maddati, visaṃ gilati.

> Kammamiccanena kvattho? Kammatthe dutiyā.

What [one] does, what [one] sees, or what [one] hears — that `kāraka` is termed `kamma` (the object).

:::tip[Examples]

> `chattaṃ karoti` \
🚻②⨀(chatta) ▶️🤟⨀(karoti) \
an umbrella | he makes

`chattaṃ` (②⨀) is the `kamma`, the thing produced.

> `rathaṃ karoti` \
🚹②⨀(ratha) ▶️🤟⨀(karoti) \
a chariot | he makes

> `rūpaṃ passati` \
🚻②⨀(rūpa) ▶️🤟⨀(passati) \
a form | he sees

> `saddaṃ suṇāti` \
🚹②⨀(sadda) ▶️🤟⨀(suṇāti) \
a sound | he hears

> `cakkhunā rūpaṃ passati … sotena saddaṃ suṇāti` \
> [(7D 10.2.9: Saṅkhadhamaupamā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/10/10.2/10.2.9#1115) \
🚻③⨀(cakkhu) 🚻②⨀(rūpa) ▶️🤟⨀(passati) 🚻③⨀(sota) 🚹②⨀(sadda) ▶️🤟⨀(suṇāti) \
with the eye | a form | he sees | with the ear | a sound | he hears

`rūpaṃ` and `saddaṃ` are the `kamma` of the `passati` and `suṇāti` clauses, `cakkhunā` and `sotena` the `karaṇa` of §279.

> `kaṇṭakaṃ maddati` \
🚹②⨀(kaṇṭaka) ▶️🤟⨀(maddati) \
a thorn | he treads on

> `visaṃ gilati` \
🚻②⨀(visa) ▶️🤟⨀(gilati) \
poison | he swallows

The thorn and the poison are objects no one wishes for; the definition covers them all the same.

:::

:::tip[Purpose]

`Kammamiccanena kvattho?` — what is the use of the term `kamma`? `Kammatthe dutiyā`: it is the term through which [§297 (284): Kammatthe dutiyā](#m297) assigns the second form ②. The Bālāvatāra (¶50) joins the two rules.

:::

---

<a name="m281"></a>

### §281 (294): Yo karoti sa kattā (the one who does: `kattā`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | yo karoti — so | (saññaṃ hoti) | kattā | | |
|        |        | the one who does — he | (is named) | `kattā` | | |

`yo` is ①⨀ "who". The one who does the action is its agent; the Rūpasiddhi (294) adds `attappadhāno`, "independent, the principal party", and divides the agent into three — the `suddhakattā` who acts himself, the `hetukattā` who makes another act ([§282 (295): Yo kāreti sa hetu](#m282)), and the `kammakattā`, an object spoken of as if it acted (`sayaṃ karīyate kaṭo`, "the mat gets made by itself"). The vutti supplies `saññā hoti`; `kattā` (`√kara` + `tu`, "doer") says what it means, so the badge is `anvattha`; there is no option marker. `kattā` is the first form of the stem `kattu`, made like `satthā` by [§199 (158): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m199). All four of the vutti's examples are passive sentences whose participle agrees with the object, so that the agent shows in the third form — the use the term serves in [§288 (293): Kattari ca](#m288); with an active verb the agent stands in the first form and needs no marking, `Kattari parassapadaṃ`, [§456 (430): Kattari parassapadaṃ](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m456). The Rūpasiddhi cites the rule at 77 and 294.

:::

> Yo karoti, so kattusañño hoti.

> Ahinā daṭṭho naro, garuḷena hato nāgo. Buddhena jito māro, upaguttena māro bandho[^32].

> Kattuiccanena kvattho? Kattari ca.

The one who does [the action] is termed `kattā` (the agent).

:::tip[Examples]

> `ahinā daṭṭho naro` \
🚹③⨀(ahi) 🚹①⨀(daṭṭha) 🚹①⨀(nara) \
by a snake | bitten | the man

`ahinā` (③⨀) is the `kattā`, the one who did the biting; `naro`, the one bitten, is the `kamma`, in ① because the participle `daṭṭho` agrees with it.

> `aññataro bhikkhu ahinā daṭṭho hoti` \
> [(2V 1.5.9.3: Vikālagāmappavisanasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.9/1.5.9.3#1508) \
🚹①⨀(aññatara) 🚹①⨀(bhikkhu) 🚹③⨀(ahi) 🚹①⨀(daṭṭha) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) \
a certain | monk | by a snake | bitten | is

> `garuḷena hato nāgo` \
🚹③⨀(garuḷa) 🚹①⨀(hata) 🚹①⨀(nāga) \
by a garuḷa | killed | the nāga

> `buddhena jito māro` \
🚹③⨀(buddha) 🚹①⨀(jita) 🚹①⨀(māra) \
by the Buddha | conquered | Māra

> `upaguttena māro bandho` \
🚹③⨀(upagutta) 🚹①⨀(māra) 🚹①⨀(bandha) \
by Upagutta | Māra | bound

The elder Upagutta's binding of Māra is a story of the Sanskrit tradition (the Aśokāvadāna), not of the Tipiṭaka.

:::

:::tip[Purpose]

`Kattuiccanena kvattho?` — what is the use of the term `kattu`? `Kattari ca`: it is the term through which [§288 (293): Kattari ca](#m288) assigns the third form ③ to the agent, and through which the Ākhyāta chapter assigns the active endings ([§456 (430): Kattari parassapadaṃ](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m456)). The Bālāvatāra (¶51) joins this definition to §288.

:::

[^32]: Nandisena's edition records the variant `baddho`. `baddha` is the regular past participle of `bandhati`, as the Vinaya spells it, and the sentence, read like the other three as a passive whose participle agrees with `māro`, expects it; `bandho` is otherwise the noun "a bond". The text keeps the CST's reading.

---

<a name="m282"></a>

### §282 (295): Yo kāreti sa hetu (the one who makes [the agent] act: `hetu`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | yo (kattāraṃ) kāreti — so | (saññaṃ hoti) | hetu (kattā ca) | | |
|        |        | the one who makes [the agent] act — he | (is named) | `hetu` (and `kattā`) | | |

`kāreti`, "makes do", is the causative of `karoti`, formed with the `ṇe` of [§438 (540): Dhātūhi ṇe ṇaya ṇāpe ṇāpayā kāritāni hetvatthe](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m438). The vutti supplies `kattāraṃ`: the one who makes the agent act — the instigator — is termed `hetu` (cause), `kattā ca`, and is an agent as well, the `hetukattā` of the Rūpasiddhi (294): `yo aññaṃ kātuṃ samatthaṃ akarontaṃ kammaṃ niyojeti`, "who sets to a task another who is able to do it but is not doing it". The name is transparent, so the badge is `anvattha`; no option marker. The person made to act keeps his own `kāraka` and may stand in the second or the third form by [§300 (286): Gati buddhi bhuja paṭha hara kara sayādīnaṃ kārite vā](#m300), or in the `sa` form of the vutti's `tassa purisassa`, which neither vutti labels and which is the sixth as much as the fourth[^33]; the vutti's three versions of the one sentence show the three. The `(M)` number: the Rūpasiddhi prints 293 for this rule, repeating the number it gives `Kattari ca` and skipping 295, and the scaffold followed that misprint; both the CST's sutta list and its vutti section print 295, which is the rule's place in the Rūpasiddhi's own sequence (291 `Karaṇe tatiyā`, 292, 293 `Kattari ca`, 294 `Yo karoti sa kattā`, 296 `Sahādiyoge ca`), so this chapter gives (295).

:::

> Yo kattāraṃ kāreti, so hetusañño hoti, kattā ca.

> So puriso taṃ purisaṃ kammaṃ kāreti so puriso tena purisena kammaṃ kāreti, so puriso tassa purisassa kammaṃ kāreti. Evaṃ hāreti pāṭheti pāceti, dhāreti.

> Hetuiccanena kvattho? Dhātūhi ṇe ṇaya ṇāpe ṇāpayā kāritāni hetvatthe.

The one who makes the agent act is termed `hetu` (the cause, the instigator) — and `kattā` (agent) too.

:::tip[Examples]

> `so puriso taṃ purisaṃ kammaṃ kāreti` \
⚧️①⨀(ta) 🚹①⨀(purisa) ⚧️②⨀(ta) 🚹②⨀(purisa) 🚻②⨀(kamma) ▶️🤟⨀(kāreti) \
that | man | that | man | the work | makes do

`so puriso` (①) is the `hetu`, and as the agent of `kāreti` stands in the first form; `taṃ purisaṃ` (②), the man made to work, is the agent of the underlying `karoti` and takes the second form by §300; `kammaṃ` (②) is the `kamma`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(purisa) | so puriso tena purisena kammaṃ kāreti | that man makes that man do the work (the one made to act in ③) |
| 🚹⑥⨀(purisa) | so puriso tassa purisassa kammaṃ kāreti | that man makes that man do the work (the one made to act in the `sa` form, read as ⑥) |

The other causatives the vutti names are formed in the same way:

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ▶️🤟⨀(√kara + ṇe + ti) | kāreti | he makes [someone] do |
| ▶️🤟⨀(√hara + ṇe + ti) | hāreti | he makes [someone] carry |
| ▶️🤟⨀(√paṭha + ṇe + ti) | pāṭheti | he makes [someone] recite |
| ▶️🤟⨀(√paca + ṇe + ti) | pāceti | he makes [someone] cook |
| ▶️🤟⨀(√dhara + ṇe + ti) | dhāreti | he makes [someone] hold |

The root vowel is lengthened before the causative suffix by [§483 (527): Asaṃyogantassa vuddhi kārite](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m483) and the `ṇ` of the suffix is dropped by [§523 (526): Kāritānaṃ ṇo lopaṃ](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m523).

:::

:::tip[Purpose]

`Hetuiccanena kvattho?` — what is the use of the term `hetu`? `Dhātūhi ṇe ṇaya ṇāpe ṇāpayā kāritāni hetvatthe`: it is the sense in which [§438 (540): Dhātūhi ṇe ṇaya ṇāpe ṇāpayā kāritāni hetvatthe](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m438) adds the four causative suffixes to a root. The `hetu` of [§289 (297): Hetvatthe ca](#m289), a reason or cause taking the third form, is a different use of the word.

:::

[^33]: `sa` is the ending of the fourth and the sixth alike ([§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55)), and neither this vutti nor that of §300 names the form. The grammar has a route only for the sixth: [§308 (317): Chaṭṭhī ca](#m308) lets it stand in the sense of the agent's third (`tena purisena`), whereas no rule makes the one made to act a `sampadāna`; the Rūpasiddhi (286) gives him only ② and ③ (`puriso purisaṃ gāmaṃ gamayati, purisena vā … evaṃ sabbattha kārite kattukammani dutiyā`), and Nandisena's edition labels `tassa purisassa` and `purisassa` the sixth. The gloss follows that reading; the fourth is the other possible label of the shape.

---

<a name="m283"></a>

### §283 (316): Yassa vā pariggaho taṃ sāmī (the one whose possession [a thing] is: `sāmī`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     |        | yassa pariggaho — taṃ | (saññaṃ hoti) | sāmī | | |
| or     |        | the one whose possession [a thing] is — that | (is named) | `sāmī` | | |

`yassa` is ⑥⨀ "whose"; `pariggaho` (`pari` + `√gah` + `a`) is what one has taken into one's own, a possession — the Rūpasiddhi: `pariggayhatīti pariggaho, yo yassa pariggaho āyatto sambandhī`, "what is possessed, dependent, related". The vutti supplies `saññaṃ hoti`. `sāmī`, "owner, master", says what it means, so the badge is `anvattha`. The owner is not strictly a `kāraka` — the Rūpasiddhi notes `kriyābhisambandhābhāvā na kārakatā sambhavati`: the relation of owner to owned is not a part in the action but the fruit of an earlier one (the king gave, the man received, hence `rañño puriso`, "the king's man") — and the vutti accordingly does not say `kārakaṃ` here. The option marker `vā` opens the term beyond ownership: the Rūpasiddhi's `vāggahaṇena sāmi-tabba-rujādiyogepi` — the constructions with `sāmī` itself, with the `tabba` participle and with `rujati` (`devadattassa rujati`, "Devadatta is in pain") — and its long list of relations that take the sixth form: whole and part (`rukkhassa sākhā`), group (`bhikkhūnaṃ samūho`), quality (`suvaṇṇassa vaṇṇo`), action (`pādassa ukkhipanaṃ`) and so on. §303–§305 add further sixth-form contexts. The Rūpasiddhi cites the rule at 91 (there spelt `sāmi`) and 316.

:::

> Yassa vā pariggaho, taṃ sāmīsaññaṃ hoti.

> Tassa bhikkhuno paṭivīso, tassa bhikkhuno patto, tassa bhikkhuno cīvaraṃ, attano mukhaṃ.

> Sāmīiccanena kvattho? Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhī.

The one whose possession [a thing] is — that is termed `sāmī` (the owner).

:::tip[Examples]

> `tassa bhikkhuno paṭivīso` \
⚧️⑥⨀(ta) 🚹⑥⨀(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨀(paṭivīsa) \
of that | monk | the share

`tassa bhikkhuno` (⑥⨀) is the `sāmī`, the owner of the share; `paṭivīso` is the possession in ①. `bhikkhuno` takes `no` for `sa` by [§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m117).

> `khādanīyassa ca bhojanīyassa ca te paṭivīso ṭhapito` \
> [(3V 1.12: Uruvelapāṭihāriyakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.12#181) \
🚻⑥⨀(khādanīya) 🔼(ca) 🚻⑥⨀(bhojanīya) 🔼(ca) ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) 🚹①⨀(paṭivīsa) 🚹①⨀(ṭhapita) \
of hard food | and | of soft food | and | your | share | was set aside

> `tassa bhikkhuno patto` \
⚧️⑥⨀(ta) 🚹⑥⨀(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨀(patta) \
of that | monk | the bowl

> `aññatarassa bhikkhuno patto bhinno hoti` \
> [(1V 1.4.3.2: Ūnapañcabandhanasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.4/1.4.3/1.4.3.2#2276) \
🚹⑥⨀(aññatara) 🚹⑥⨀(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨀(patta) 🚹①⨀(bhinna) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) \
of a certain | monk | the bowl | broken | is

> `tassa bhikkhuno cīvaraṃ` \
⚧️⑥⨀(ta) 🚹⑥⨀(bhikkhu) 🚻①⨀(cīvara) \
of that | monk | the robe

> `attano mukhaṃ` \
🚹⑥⨀(atta) 🚻①⨀(mukha) \
of oneself | the face

`attano` is the sixth form of `atta` by [§213 (127): Sassa no](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m213).

:::

:::tip[Purpose]

`Sāmīiccanena kvattho?` — what is the use of the term `sāmī`? `Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhī`: it is the term through which [§301 (315): Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhī](#m301) assigns the sixth form ⑥, whose vutti repeats these four examples. The Bālāvatāra (¶56) joins the two rules.

:::

---

<a name="m284"></a>

### §284 (283): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā (the first form ① for the bare meaning of the stem) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | liṅgatthe (abhidhānamatte) | (vibhatti hoti) | paṭhamā | | |
|        |        | in [merely] expressing the meaning of the stem | (the inflection is) | the first ① | | |

`liṅga` is the nominal stem, the lemma that [§53 (61): Liṅgañca nippajjate](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m53) derives and to which [§54 (62): Tato ca vibhattiyo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m54) adds the inflections; `liṅgattha` is its meaning — the thing named, with its gender and number, before it takes any part in a sentence as `kamma`, `kattā` or the rest. The vutti's `abhidhānamatte`, "in the mere expression", says so, and the Bālāvatāra (¶48) spells it out: `yo kammakattādivattantaramappatto sassarūpaṭṭho suddho, so liṅgattho nāma`. With this rule the chapter turns from defining the `kāraka` (§271–§283) to assigning the inflection forms to them and to further senses (§284–§315): ① here and in §285, ③ in §286–§292, ④ in §293–§294, ⑤ in §295–§296, ② in §297–§300, ⑥ in §301, ⑦ in §302, then the mixed rules. The forms themselves are the `si` and `yo` of [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55). The rule has no marker and no restriction: it is the general rule (`utsarga`) for the first form, the one the Bālāvatāra starts its inflection tables from, and the Rūpasiddhi cites it at 65 as well as 283. The vutti's examples take in the numerals `eko`, `dve` and six particles: a particle names its meaning and nothing more, and so has a first form, which is then elided by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221).

:::

> Liṅgatthābhidhānamatte paṭhamāvibhatti hoti.

> Puriso, purisā, eko, dve, ca, vā, he, ahe, re, are.

In the mere expression of the meaning of the stem (`liṅgattha`)[^34], the first inflection form ① is used.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(purisa) | puriso | a man |
| 🚹①⨂(purisa) | purisā | men |
| ⚧️①⨀(eka) | eko | one |
| ⚧️①⨂(dvi) | dve | two |
| 🔼(ca) | ca | and |
| 🔼(vā) | vā | or |
| 🔼(he) | he | hey! |
| 🔼(ahe) | ahe | friend! |
| 🔼(re) | re | hey, you! |
| 🔼(are) | are | away with you! |

|     | purisa + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (si→o)        | §104 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + o)    | §83  |
| →   | **puriso**             |      |

|     | purisa + yo (①⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (yo→ā)        | §107 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + ā)    | §83  |
| →   | **purisā**             |      |

|     | dvi + yo (①⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (dvi + yo → dve)       | §132 |
| →   | **dve**                |      |

`puriso` is the first form of a `kattā` only once a verb is added (`puriso gacchati`); by itself it merely names a man, and that is the `liṅgattha`. `eko` is `eka` + `si` with the `o` of [§104 (66): So](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m104), `dve` the form of [§132 (228): Yosu dvinnaṃ dve ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m132); for the particles the Rūpasiddhi (283) says the first form stands `liṅgādivinimutte sattāmatte`, "in the mere being of what is free of gender and the rest", and it is elided as soon as it is added.

:::

:::note[Additional]

The Bālāvatāra (¶48) takes the occasion to define grammatical number, `vacana`: the expression of one thing is `ekavacana` (⨀) and of many `bahuvacana` (⨂). The Rūpasiddhi (283) adds that a meaning already expressed by a verb, a `kita` or `taddhita` suffix or a compound (`abhihita`) is likewise a bare `liṅgattha` and takes the first form — which is why the object of `sugatena desito dhammo` is `dhammo`, not `dhammaṃ`.

:::

[^34]: U Nandisena (2005) renders `liṅgattha` "the property of the stem". `attha` is the meaning or referent: the Bālāvatāra's gloss (¶48, `yo kammakattādivattantaramappatto sassarūpaṭṭho suddho, so liṅgattho nāma`) and the Rūpasiddhi's `sattāmatte`, "in the mere being", both speak of the bare referent, not of a property.

---

<a name="m285"></a>

### §285 (70): Ālapane ca (and in address ⓪) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | ālapane (ālapanatthādhike liṅgatthābhidhānamatte) | (vibhatti hoti) | (paṭhamā) | | |
| and    |        | in address — the meaning of the stem with the sense of address added | (the inflection is) | (the first ①, shown as ⓪) | | |

`paṭhamāvibhatti hoti` and `liṅgatthābhidhānamatte` continue from [§284 (283): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā](#m284); the vutti's `ālapanatthādhike` is `ālapana-attha-adhike`, "with the sense of address in addition". By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §284: the first form serves also when the bare meaning is spoken *to* — `ālapana`, "speaking to", the Rūpasiddhi's `abhimukhaṃ katvā lapanaṃ`, "speaking face to face", a summons (`āmantana`). It is not a `kāraka`: the Rūpasiddhi notes that at the moment of address the person is not yet joined to any action (`kriyāyogābhāvato idaṃ kārakavohāraṃ na labhati`). The site shows this use of the first form as ⓪. Its singular `si` is then renamed `ga` by [§57 (71): Ālapane si ga sañño](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m57), and the `ga` sets off the vocative changes: `ā` for a final `a` by [§244 (72): Akārapitādyantānamā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m244), optionally shortened again by [§246 (73): Ākāro vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m246), the `ga` itself elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m220), `e` for `sakha` by [§113 (132): Sakhato gasse vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m113), `bho` for `bhavanta` by [§243 (110): Bho ge tu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m243). The plural `yo` keeps its first-form shapes (`purisā`, `rājāno`, `sakhino`). No restriction attaches beyond the `ca`: as the general assignment of a form to the vocative it is `utsarga`. The `(M)` 70 is the Rūpasiddhi's Nāma-section citation; it does not repeat the rule in its Kāraka section. The Bālāvatāra treats it at ¶49.

:::

> Ālapanatthā dhike liṅgatthābhidhānamatte ca paṭhamāvibhatti hoti.

> Bho purisa, bhavanto purisā, bho rāja, bhavanto rājāno, he sakhe, he sakhino.

When the meaning of the stem is expressed with the sense of address (`ālapana`) added, the first inflection form ① is used as well.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(bhavanta) 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | bho purisa | sir, man! |
| 🚹⓪⨂(bhavanta) 🚹⓪⨂(purisa) | bhavanto purisā | sirs, men! |
| 🚹⓪⨀(bhavanta) 🚹⓪⨀(rāja) | bho rāja | sir, king! |
| 🚹⓪⨂(bhavanta) 🚹⓪⨂(rāja) | bhavanto rājāno | sirs, kings! |
| 🔼(he) 🚹⓪⨀(sakha) | he sakhe | hey, friend! |
| 🔼(he) 🚹⓪⨂(sakha) | he sakhino | hey, friends! |

|     | purisa + ga (⓪⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (a→ā) + ga     | §244 |
| →   | purisā + ~~ga~~        | §220 |
| →   | puris + (ā→a)          | §246 |
| →   | **purisa**             |      |

|     | sakha + ga (⓪⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakha + (ga→e)         | §113 |
| →   | sakh + (~~a~~ + e)     | §83  |
| →   | **sakhe**              |      |

> `ehi tvaṃ, bho purisa` \
> [(8D 1.5: Iddhipāṭihāriyakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/8D/1/1.5#44) \
⏯🤘⨀(eti) ⚧️①⨀(tumha) 🚹⓪⨀(bhavanta) 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) \
come | you | sir | man

`bho` is the vocative of `bhavanta` by §243, `bhavanto` its plural by [§122 (99): Ntussa nto](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m122) read with [§187 (108): Sesesu ntuva](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m187); `rājāno` is the `āno` of [§190 (114): Yonamāno](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m190), `sakhino` the `no` of [§191 (130): Sakhato cāyo no](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m191) with `i` for the final by [§194 (131): Sakhantassi no nā naṃ sesu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m194). The particle `he` is one of the six §284 lists.

:::

---

<a name="m286"></a>

### §286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā (the third form ③ for the instrument) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | karaṇe (kārake) | (vibhatti hoti) | tatiyā | | |
|        |        | for the `karaṇa` (instrument) | (the inflection is) | the third ③ | | |

`karaṇe` is the locative of the term defined in [§279 (292): Yena vā kayirate taṃ karaṇaṃ](#m279): for that `kāraka` the third form is used — `nā` in the singular and `hi` in the plural by [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55), `nā` becoming `ena` after `a` by [§103 (79): Ato nena](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m103). No marker and no restriction: the general rule (`utsarga`) of the third form, to which the six `ca` rules that follow (§287–§292) add further uses. The Rūpasiddhi cites it at 83 as well as 291 and, following the Bālāvatāra's order, treats the agent first (`Kattari ca`, 293) and the instrument as its equal — `sabbaṃ kattusamaṃ`, the Bālāvatāra says at ¶52, "all the forms are the same as the agent's".

:::

> Karaṇakārake tatiyāvibhatti hoti.

> Agginā kuṭiṃ jhāpeti, manasā ce paduṭṭhena, manasā ce pasannena, kāyena kammaṃ karoti.

For the `karaṇa` (instrument) `kāraka` the third inflection form ③ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `agginā kuṭiṃ jhāpeti` \
🚹③⨀(aggi) 🚺②⨀(kuṭi) ▶️🤟⨀(jhāpeti) \
with fire | the hut | he burns

`agginā` (③⨀) is the `karaṇa`; `kuṭiṃ` is the `kamma` in ② by [§297 (284): Kammatthe dutiyā](#m297).

> `manasā ce paduṭṭhena` \
> [(18Dh 1.1: Cakkhupālattheravatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/1/1.1#2) \
🚹③⨀(mana) 🔼(ce) 🚹③⨀(paduṭṭha) \
with a mind | if | corrupted

> `manasā ce pasannena` \
> [(18Dh 1.2: Maṭṭhakuṇḍalīvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/1/1.2#3) \
🚹③⨀(mana) 🔼(ce) 🚹③⨀(pasanna) \
with a mind | if | serene

`manasā`, the instrument of the speaking and acting of the two Dhammapada verses, is the `mano`-group's third form: `ā` for `nā` by [§181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m181) with the inserted `s` of [§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m184); `paduṭṭhena` and `pasannena` agree with it.

> `kāyena kammaṃ karoti` \
> [(10M 2.1: Ambalaṭṭhikarāhulovādasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/2/2.1#288) \
🚹③⨀(kāya) 🚻②⨀(kamma) ▶️🤟⨀(karoti) \
with the body | a deed | he does

The mind and the body are the Rūpasiddhi's internal (`ajjhattika`) instruments, the fire its external (`bāhira`) one.

:::

---

<a name="m287"></a>

### §287 (296): Sahādiyoge ca (and in construction with `saha` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | sahādiyoge (sahādiyogatthe) | (vibhatti hoti) | (tatiyā) | | |
| and    |        | in the sense of construction with `saha` and the like | (the inflection is) | (the third ③) | | |

The sutta is `saha-ādi-yoge ca`: `saha`, "together with", and its like — `saddhiṃ` ("with"), `samaṃ` ("equal to"), `vinā` ("without"); the Rūpasiddhi (296) adds `nānā`, `alaṃ` and `kiṃ` (`kiṃ te jaṭāhi dummedha`, "what use are your matted locks, fool?"). `tatiyāvibhatti hoti` continues from [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286), and by saying `ca` this rule is an extension of it: the word construed with `saha` and its kind takes the third form though it is no instrument — the companion of the Saṅgha's act, the standard of a comparison. The word so construed is not one of the six `kāraka`; the rule names a context, so it is `apavada`. `vinā` also takes the fifth and the second by the `api` of [§272 (309): Dhātunāmānamupasaggayogādīsvapi ca](#m272), where the same word stands among the particles: the vutti there gives `vinā buddhasmā, vinā buddhaṃ, vinā buddhena vā`, and the Rūpasiddhi's `ca` here also covers the sense "with" when no particle is expressed (`kokālikena pacchāsamaṇena`, "with Kokālika as attendant")[^35]. The `(M)` is 296 in the CST's sutta list and the Rūpasiddhi; the CST's vutti section prints (299).

:::

> Sahādiyogatthe ca tatiyāvibhatti hoti.

> Sahāpi gaggena saṅgho uposathaṃ kareyya, vināpi gaggena, mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ, sahassena samaṃ mitā.

In the sense of construction with `saha` ("together with") and the like, too, the third inflection form ③ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `sahāpi gaggena saṅgho uposathaṃ kareyya` \
🔼(saha) 🔼(api) 🚹③⨀(gagga) 🚹①⨀(saṅgha) 🚹②⨀(uposatha) ⏯🤟⨀(karoti) \
together with | even | Gagga | the Saṅgha | the uposatha | may perform

`gaggena` (③⨀) takes the third form because it is construed with `saha`; `saṅgho` is the `kattā` in ① and `uposathaṃ` the `kamma` in ②.

> `vināpi gaggena` \
🔼(vinā) 🔼(api) 🚹③⨀(gagga) \
without | even | Gagga

> `saṃgho saha vā gaggena vinā vā gaggena uposathaṃ kareyya` \
> [(3V 2.23: Ummattakasammuti)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/2/2.23#697) \
🚹①⨀(saṅgha) 🔼(saha) 🔼(vā) 🚹③⨀(gagga) 🔼(vinā) 🔼(vā) 🚹③⨀(gagga) 🚹②⨀(uposatha) ⏯🤟⨀(karoti) \
the Saṅgha | with | or | Gagga | without | or | Gagga | the uposatha | may perform

> `mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ` \
🚹③⨀(mahanta) 🚹③⨀(bhikkhusaṅgha) 🔼(saddhiṃ) \
with a great | community of monks | together

`mahatā` is the `tā` for `nā` of [§127 (102): To titā sasmiṃnāsu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m127), which `mahanta` follows by [§187 (108): Sesesu ntuva](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m187).

> `appasmā dakkhiṇā dinnā, sahassena samaṃ mitā` \
> [(12S1 1.4.2: Maccharisutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/1/1.4/1.4.2#150) \
🚻⑤⨀(appa) 🚺①⨀(dakkhiṇā) 🚺①⨀(dinna) 🚻③⨀(sahassa) 🔼(samaṃ) 🚺①⨀(mita) \
from little | a gift | given | with a thousand | equal | is measured

`sahassena` is construed with `samaṃ`, "equal to": the gift is weighed as equal to a thousand.

:::

[^35]: The Nyāsa divides the vutti's `sahādiyogatthe` as `sahādiyoge atthe ca` — `sahādīnaṃ yoge sati, tadatthe ca tatiyāvibhatti hoti`, the third form both in construction with `saha` and its kind and in their sense alone — as Nandisena's edition records; the division is the traditional warrant for this extension.

---

<a name="m288"></a>

### §288 (293): Kattari ca (and for the agent) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | kattari (kārake) | (vibhatti hoti) | (tatiyā) | | |
| and    |        | for the `kattā` (agent) | (the inflection is) | (the third ③) | | |

`kattari` is the locative of `kattu`, the term defined in [§281 (294): Yo karoti sa kattā](#m281), made like `satthari` by [§200 (159): Aññesvārattaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m200), [§206 (165): Tato smimi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m206) and [§208 (166): Āro rassamikāre](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m208). `tatiyāvibhatti hoti` continues from [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286), and by saying `ca` the rule extends the third form to the agent. It applies when the agent is not already expressed (`abhihita`) by the verb's own agreement: with a passive verb or a past participle the agent stands in the third form (`raññā hato poso`), while with an active verb it stands in the first (`rājā posaṃ hanati`), which is why the Rūpasiddhi (293) says `abhihite na bhavati` and the Ākhyāta chapter gives the agent the active endings ([§456 (430): Kattari parassapadaṃ](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m456)). Within that condition the assignment is general and unrestricted, so the badge is `utsarga` — the Bālāvatāra (¶51) even makes it the primary rule of the third form and adds the instrument afterwards. The Rūpasiddhi cites it at 78 and 293, and gives its `ca` a longer reach: the third form also for a distinguishing mark (`itthambhūtalakkhaṇa`: `bhinnena sīsena`, "with a broken head"), for the time an action takes to complete (`navahi māsehi vihāraṃ niṭṭhāpesi`), with `pubba`, `sadisa`, `sama`, `ūna`, `kalaha`, `nipuṇa`, `missaka`, `sakhila` and `attha` (`maṇinā attho`), for time and distance (`māsena bhuñjati`), and in the sense of the second and fifth forms — uses the Kaccāyana distributes over §289–§292 and §277.

:::

> Kattari ca tatiyāvibhatti hoti.

> Raññā hato poso, yakkhena dinno varo, ahinā daṭṭho naro.

For the `kattā` (agent) too the third inflection form ③ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `raññā hato poso` \
🚹③⨀(rāja) 🚹①⨀(hata) 🚹①⨀(posa) \
by the king | killed | the man

`raññā` (③⨀) is the `kattā`; the participle `hato` agrees with `poso`, the `kamma`, which therefore stands in ①. `raññā` is the form of [§137 (116): Nāmhi raññā vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m137).

> `yakkhena dinno varo` \
🚹③⨀(yakkha) 🚹①⨀(dinna) 🚹①⨀(vara) \
by the yakkha | given | the boon

> `ahinā daṭṭho naro` \
🚹③⨀(ahi) 🚹①⨀(daṭṭha) 🚹①⨀(nara) \
by a snake | bitten | the man

> `tena kho pana samayena aññataro bhikkhu ahinā daṭṭho hoti` \
> [(2V 1.5.9.3: Vikālagāmappavisanasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.9/1.5.9.3#1508) \
⚧️③⨀(ta) 🔼(kho) 🔼(pana) 🚹③⨀(samaya) 🚹①⨀(aññatara) 🚹①⨀(bhikkhu) 🚹③⨀(ahi) 🚹①⨀(daṭṭha) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) \
at that | indeed | now | time | a certain | monk | by a snake | bitten | is

The same sentence shows the third form in the sense of the seventh, `tena samayena`, which [§290 (298): Sattamyatthe ca](#m290) provides for.

:::

---

<a name="m289"></a>

### §289 (297): Hetvatthe ca (and in the sense of cause) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | hetvatthe | (vibhatti hoti) | (tatiyā) | | |
| and    |        | in the sense of `hetu` (cause) | (the inflection is) | (the third ③) | | |

`hetvatthe` is `hetu` + `atthe`, the `u` becoming `v` before the vowel by [§18 (20): Vamodudantānaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m18). `hetu` here is a reason or cause (`kāraṇa`), not the instigating agent of [§282 (295): Yo kāreti sa hetu](#m282): "he stays *because of* food, *because of* the Dhamma" — the Rūpasiddhi (297): `kismiñci phale diṭṭhasāmatthiyaṃ kāraṇaṃ hetu`, "a cause whose efficacy is seen in some result". `tatiyāvibhatti hoti` continues from [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286), and by saying `ca` the rule extends the third form to this sense; since it names a sense rather than a `kāraka`, it is `apavada`. The cause has other forms too: the fifth by [§296 (314): Kāraṇatthe ca](#m296) (`ananubodhā appaṭivedhā`, "through not understanding, through not penetrating") and, in the pronouns, by the `hetvatthe` of [§275 (312): Dūranti kaddha kāla nimmāna tvālopa disāyogavibhattārappayoga suddhappamocana hetu vivittappamāṇa pubbayogabandhana guṇavacana pañha kathana thokākattūsu ca](#m275) (`kasmā`), beside the third (`kena hetunā`) and sixth (`kissa hetu`) there.

:::

> Hetvatthe ca tatiyāvibhatti hoti.

> Annena vasati, dhammena vasati, vijjāya vasati, sakkārena vasati.

In the sense of cause (`hetu`), too, the third inflection form ③ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `annena vasati` \
🚻③⨀(anna) ▶️🤟⨀(vasati) \
because of food | he stays

`annena` (③⨀) is the cause of his staying — he stays where he does on account of the food he gets there; it is neither the instrument of an act nor its agent. `vasati` is "dwells, stays", and the third form gives the reason for staying, not the means of subsistence.

> `dhammena vasati` \
🚹③⨀(dhamma) ▶️🤟⨀(vasati) \
because of the Dhamma | he stays

> `vijjāya vasati` \
🚺③⨀(vijjā) ▶️🤟⨀(vasati) \
because of knowledge | he stays

> `sakkārena vasati` \
🚹③⨀(sakkāra) ▶️🤟⨀(vasati) \
because of honour | he stays

`vijjāya` is the feminine third form, `āya` for `nā` by [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m111). The Rūpasiddhi (297) adds the canonical

> `na jaccā vasalo hoti, na jaccā hoti brāhmaṇo; kammunā vasalo hoti, kammunā hoti brāhmaṇo` \
> [(18Sn 1.7: Vasalasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Sn/1/1.7#156) \
🔼(na) 🚺③⨀(jāti) 🚹①⨀(vasala) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🔼(na) 🚺③⨀(jāti) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚹①⨀(brāhmaṇa) 🚻③⨀(kamma) 🚹①⨀(vasala) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚻③⨀(kamma) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚹①⨀(brāhmaṇa) \
not | by birth | an outcaste | one is | not | by birth | one is | a brahmin | by action | an outcaste | one is | by action | one is | a brahmin

where `jaccā` and `kammunā` are the cause: `kammunā` takes `u` for the final of `kamma` before `nā` by [§160 (197): A kammantassa ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m160).

:::

---

<a name="m290"></a>

### §290 (298): Sattamyatthe ca (and in the sense of the seventh) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | sattamyatthe | (vibhatti hoti) | (tatiyā) | | |
| and    |        | in the sense of the seventh ⑦ | (the inflection is) | (the third ③) | | |

`sattamyatthe` is `sattamī` + `atthe`, the `ī` becoming `y` before the vowel by [§21 (21): Ivaṇṇo yaṃ navā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m21). `tatiyāvibhatti hoti` continues from [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286), and by saying `ca` the rule extends the third form to the sense that the seventh form usually expresses — the time at which: `tena samayena`, "at that time", for `tasmiṃ samaye`. The Rūpasiddhi (298) widens it to time, distance, direction and place (`kāladdhānadisādesādīsu`): `māsena bhuñjati` ("he eats once a month"), `yojanena dhāvati`, `puratthimena dhataraṭṭho` ("in the east, Dhataraṭṭha"), `yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami` ("where the Blessed One was, there he approached"). A sense-rule, not a `kāraka` rule, hence `apavada`. The seventh form itself remains available — [§302 (319): Okāse sattamī](#m302) — and the second form serves the same sense by [§307 (290): Tatiyāsattamīnañca](#m307) (`ekaṃ samayaṃ`). The CST prints `(Yena kālena, yena samayena,)` in parentheses, marking the two phrases as an addition to its text[^36]; they are the relative pair of the demonstrative `tena kālena, tena samayena`.

:::

> Sattamyatthe ca tatiyāvibhatti hoti.

> Tena kālena, tena samayena. (Yena kālena, yena samayena,) tena kho pana samayena.

In the sense of the seventh form ⑦, too, the third inflection form ③ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `tena kālena` \
> [(20Ap1 1.8: Upālittheraapadāna)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/20Ap1/1/1.8#465) \
⚧️③⨀(ta) 🚹③⨀(kāla) \
at that | time

`kālena` (③⨀) names the time of the action, the sense of the seventh form (`tasmiṃ kāle`); it is not the instrument.

> `tena samayena buddho bhagavā rājagahe viharati … tena kho pana samayena sambahulā … bhikkhū` \
> [(1V 1.1.2: Dutiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2#191) \
⚧️③⨀(ta) 🚹③⨀(samaya) 🚹①⨀(buddha) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) 🚻⑦⨀(rājagaha) ▶️🤟⨀(viharati) ⚧️③⨀(ta) 🔼(kho) 🔼(pana) 🚹③⨀(samaya) 🚹①⨂(sambahula) 🚹①⨂(bhikkhu) \
at that | time | the Buddha | the Blessed One | at Rājagaha | dwells | at that | indeed | now | time | many | monks

`rājagahe` in the same sentence shows the seventh form proper for the place.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨀(ya) 🚹③⨀(kāla) | yena kālena | at which time |
| ⚧️③⨀(ya) 🚹③⨀(samaya) | yena samayena | at which time |
| ⚧️③⨀(ta) 🚹③⨀(samaya) | tena kho pana samayena | now at that time |

:::

[^36]: Nandisena's edition records that the bracketed phrases are wanting in the Sinhalese text (`Sīhaḷapotthake natthi`), the ground for the CST's parentheses.

---

<a name="m291"></a>

### §291 (299): Yenaṅgavikāro (the limb by which a bodily defect is marked) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | yena (aṅgena aṅgino vikāro lakkhīyate) — tattha | (vibhatti hoti) | (tatiyā) | | |
|        |        | the limb by which the defect of its owner is marked — there | (the inflection is) | (the third ③) | | |

The sutta is `yena aṅgavikāro`, the first `a` of `aṅgavikāro` elided after `yena` by [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12). `aṅga` is a limb, `aṅgī` the person who has it (`aṅgamassa atthīti aṅgaṃ, sarīraṃ`, the Rūpasiddhi glosses, "the body"), `vikāra` a deformity; the vutti's `byādhimatā aṅgena`, "by a diseased limb" (③⨀ of `byādhimantu`), names the limb whose affliction shows as the person's defect: blind in the eye, lame in the foot — the Sanskrit grammarians' `yenāṅgavikāraḥ`, `akṣṇā kāṇaḥ`. `tatiyāvibhatti hoti` continues from [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286); the vutti's `tattha`, "there", points to that limb. There is no `ca` and no option marker — a separate rule for one context, obligatory within it, so `apavada`.

:::

> Yena byādhimatā aṅgena aṅgino vikāro lakkhīyate. Tattha tatiyāvibhatti hoti.

> Akkhinā kāṇo, hatthena kuṇī, kāṇaṃ passati nettena, pādena khañjo, piṭṭhiyā khujjo.

By which diseased limb the defect of the one who has the limb is marked — for that [limb] the third inflection form ③ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `akkhinā kāṇo` \
🚻③⨀(akkhi) 🚹①⨀(kāṇa) \
in the eye | one-eyed

`akkhinā` (③⨀) is the limb whose defect makes its owner `kāṇo`; the adjective of the defect stands in ① with the person.

> `hatthena kuṇī` \
🚹③⨀(hattha) 🚹①⨀(kuṇī) \
in the hand | crippled

> `kāṇaṃ passati nettena` \
🚻②⨀(kāṇa) ▶️🤟⨀(passati) 🚻③⨀(netta) \
one-eyed | he sees | with the eye

Here the defect is put as an adverb (`kāṇaṃ`, "in a one-eyed way") and the limb still takes the third form.[^37]

> `pādena khañjo` \
🚹③⨀(pāda) 🚹①⨀(khañja) \
in the foot | lame

> `piṭṭhiyā khujjo` \
🚺③⨀(piṭṭhi) 🚹①⨀(khujja) \
in the back | hunchbacked

`piṭṭhiyā` is the feminine `yā` of [§112 (183): Pato yā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m112). The adjectives are the Vinaya's list of those not to be given the going forth:

> `na, bhikkhave, kāṇo pabbājetabbo … na, bhikkhave, kuṇī pabbājetabbo … na, bhikkhave, khañjo pabbājetabbo` \
> [(3V 1.57: Napabbājetabbadvattiṃsavāra)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.57#502) \
🔼(na) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨀(kāṇa) 🚹①⨀(pabbājetabba) 🔼(na) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨀(kuṇī) 🚹①⨀(pabbājetabba) 🔼(na) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨀(khañja) 🚹①⨀(pabbājetabba) \
not | monks | a one-eyed man | is to be given the going forth | not | monks | a cripple | is to be given the going forth | not | monks | a lame man | is to be given the going forth

:::

[^37]: U Nandisena (2005) takes `kāṇaṃ` as the person seen, "he sees a person that is blind by eye". On that construal `nettena` is a plain instrument of seeing, the `karaṇa` of [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286), and the sentence no longer illustrates this rule; only the adverbial reading keeps `nettena` the limb by which the defect is marked, though the sentence is awkward on any reading.

---

<a name="m292"></a>

### §292 (300): Visesane ca (and for the distinguishing attribute) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | visesane (visesanatthe) | (vibhatti hoti) | (tatiyā) | | |
| and    |        | in the sense of `visesana` (a distinguishing attribute) | (the inflection is) | (the third ③) | | |

`visesana` is "what distinguishes" — the Rūpasiddhi (300): `visesīyati visesitabbaṃ anenāti visesanaṃ, gottādi`, "that by which the thing to be distinguished is distinguished: the clan and the like" — the respect in which a person is specified: clan, name, birth, craft, age, quality (`gotta-nāma-jāti-sippa-vayo-guṇa`). `tatiyāvibhatti hoti` continues from [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286), and by saying `ca` the rule extends the third form to this sense: Gotama *by clan*, handsome *in complexion*, supreme *in austerity*. The Rūpasiddhi's `ca` adds `pakati` and the like (`pakatiyā abhirūpo`, "handsome by nature"; `sahassena assake kiṇāti`, "he buys horses at a thousand"). A sense-rule for a named context, hence `apavada`; it is the last of the third-form rules, and the fourth form begins with [§293 (85, 301): Sampadāne catutthī](#m293).

:::

> Visesanatthe ca tatiyāvibhatti hoti.

> Gottena gotamo nātho, suvaṇṇena abhirūpo, tapasā uttamo.

In the sense of a distinguishing attribute (`visesana`), too, the third inflection form ③ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `gottena gotamo nātho` \
🚻③⨀(gotta) 🚹①⨀(gotama) 🚹①⨀(nātha) \
by clan | Gotama | the Protector

`gottena` (③⨀) is the respect in which the Protector is distinguished as a Gotama.

> `vipassī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho koṇḍañño gottena ahosi` \
> [(7D 1.1: Pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/1/1.1#8) \
🚹①⨀(vipassī) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) 🚹①⨀(arahanta) 🚹①⨀(sammāsambuddha) 🚹①⨀(koṇḍañña) 🚻③⨀(gotta) ⏮🤟⨀(hoti) \
Vipassī | monks | the Blessed One | the Arahant | the fully awakened | a Koṇḍañña | by clan | was

> `suvaṇṇena abhirūpo` \
🚻③⨀(suvaṇṇa) 🚹①⨀(abhirūpa) \
in complexion | handsome

`suvaṇṇa` here is "good colour", the complexion, not gold.

> `tapasā uttamo` \
🚻③⨀(tapa) 🚹①⨀(uttama) \
in austerity | supreme

`tapasā` is the `mano`-group's third form, like `manasā` in [§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā](#m286): `ā` for `nā` by [§181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m181) and the inserted `s` of [§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m184).

:::

---

<a name="m293"></a>

### §293 (85, 301): Sampadāne catutthī (④ for the `sampadāna` [recipient]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (liṅgamhā) |    | (hoti)    | catutthī (vibhatti) | | sampadāne (kārake) |
|        | (after the stem) | | (is used) | the fourth form ④ | | for the `sampadāna` (recipient) |

`sampadāne` is the seventh form of `sampadāna`, the kāraka defined at [§276 (302): Yassa dātukāmo rocate dhārayate vā taṃ sampadānaṃ](#m276) — the one to whom a giver wishes to give, whom a thing pleases, or for whom it is held — whose vutti pointed forward to this rule (`Sampadānamiccanena kvattho? Sampadāne catutthī`). `catutthī` ("the fourth") is the fourth of the seven inflection forms of [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55), the pair `sa`, `naṃ`; the vutti supplies `kārake` and `vibhatti`. There is no option marker: the fourth is the general expression of the recipient, and the rules that follow work on it — [§294 (305): Namoyogādīsvapi ca](#m294) extends it to `namo` and the like, [§311 (325): Sampadāne ca](#m311) lets the seventh stand in its place, and the long list of [§277 (303): Silāgha hanu ṭhā sapa dhāra piha kudha duhisso ssūya rādhikkha paccāsuṇa anupatigiṇa pubbakattārocanattha tadattha tumatthālamattha maññānādarappāṇini gatyatthakammaniāsisatthasammutibhiyyasattamyatthesu ca](#m277) has already widened the term `sampadāna` itself; the `purisassa` that [§300 (286): Gati buddhi bhuja paṭha hara kara sayādīnaṃ kārite vā](#m300) allows for the one made to act in a causative has the shape of the fourth but is read there as the sixth. The singular `sa` takes the inserted `s` of [§61 (86): Sāgamo se](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m61) and, after an `a` stem, may instead become `āya` by [§109 (304): Āya catutthekavacanassatu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m109). The Bālāvatāra states the rule at ¶53. The Padarūpasiddhi states the sutta at 85, among the noun rules that introduce each form in turn, and again at 301 in its kāraka section, and the CST's sutta list and vutti section both print 301 — hence the two numbers, as for [§271 (88, 308): Yasmā dapeti bhayamādatte vā tadapādānaṃ](#m271).

:::

> Sampadānakārake catutthīvibhatti hoti.

> Buddhassa vā dhammassa vā saṅghassa vā dānaṃ deti, dātā hoti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā.

In the `sampadāna` kāraka (the recipient) the fourth inflection form ④ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `buddhassa vā dhammassa vā saṅghassa vā dānaṃ deti` \
🚹④⨀(buddha) 🔼(vā) 🚹④⨀(dhamma) 🔼(vā) 🚹④⨀(saṅgha) 🔼(vā) 🚻②⨀(dāna) ▶️🤟⨀(deti) \
to the Buddha | or | to the Dhamma | or | to the Saṅgha | or | a gift | one gives

`buddhassa`, `dhammassa` and `saṅghassa` are the recipients of the giving, the `sampadāna`, and stand in ④⨀; `dānaṃ`, the thing given, is the `kamma` in ② by [§297 (284): Kammatthe dutiyā](#m297).

> `dātā hoti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā` \
> [(11M 4.5: Cūḷakammavibhaṅgasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/11M/4/4.5#887) \
🚹①⨀(dātu) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚹④⨀(samaṇa) 🔼(vā) 🚹④⨀(brāhmaṇa) 🔼(vā) \
a giver | he is | to an ascetic | or | to a brahmin | or

Here the recipient depends not on a finite verb but on the agent noun `dātā` ("giver"): `samaṇassa` and `brāhmaṇassa` are the `sampadāna` in ④⨀.

|     | buddha + sa (④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | buddha + (s) + sa      | §61  |
| →   | **buddhassa**          |      |

By [§109 (304): Āya catutthekavacanassatu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m109) the same `sa` may instead become `āya`: `buddhāya`.

:::

---

<a name="m294"></a>

### §294 (305): Namoyogādīsvapi ca (and ④ in construction with `namo` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| api ca | (liṅgamhā) |    | (hoti)    | (catutthī vibhatti) | | namoyogādīsu |
| also, and | (after the stem) | | (is used) | (the fourth form ④) | | in construction with `namo` and the like |

The sutta is `namo-yoga-ādīsu api ca`: `yoga` is "construction with", `ādi` "and the like", `api` "also". `catutthī` continues from [§293 (85, 301): Sampadāne catutthī](#m293). By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §293, and `api` adds the further use: the fourth form is used not only for the recipient kāraka but also for the person or thing to which `namo` ("homage") and the words of its kind are addressed — the vutti's examples add `sotthi` ("well-being") and `svāgataṃ` ("welcome"), and the Rūpasiddhi (305) names `sotthi` and `svāgata` as the `ādi`. Nothing is given to such a person, so the definition of `sampadāna` at [§276 (302): Yassa dātukāmo rocate dhārayate vā taṃ sampadānaṃ](#m276) does not reach these constructions and the extension is needed. There is no option marker: with these words the fourth is the form.

:::

> Namoyogādīsvapi ca catutthīvibhatti hoti.

> Namo te buddha vīratthu, sotthi pajānaṃ, namo karohi nāgassa, svāgataṃ te mahārāja.

And also in construction with `namo` and the like the fourth inflection form ④ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `namo te buddha vīratthu` \
> [(12S1 2.1.9: Candimasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/2/2.1/2.1.9#413) \
🔼(namo) ⚧️④⨀(tumha) 🚹⓪⨀(buddha) 🚹⓪⨀(vīra) ⏯🤟⨀(atthi) \
homage | to you | O Buddha | O hero | let there be

`te` is the fourth form of `tumha`, the enclitic of [§148 (247): Temekavacanesu ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m148), governed by `namo`; `vīratthu` is `vīra atthu`, the vocative and the imperative of `atthi`.

> `sotthi pajānaṃ` \
🚺①⨀(sotthi) 🚺④⨂(pajā) \
well-being | to living beings

`pajānaṃ` is the fourth form plural, governed by `sotthi`; the phrase is the grammarians' own.

> `namo karohi nāgassa` \
> [(9M 3.3: Vammikasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/9M/3/3.3#838) \
🔼(namo) ⏯🤘⨀(karoti) 🚹④⨀(nāga) \
homage | make | to the nāga

`nāgassa` is the fourth form governed by `namo`, here itself the object of `karohi` ("pay homage").

> `svāgataṃ te mahārāja` \
> [(7D 4.2.1: Cakkaratana)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/4/4.2/4.2.1#541) \
🚻①⨀(svāgata) ⚧️④⨀(tumha) 🚹⓪⨀(mahārāja) \
welcome | to you | great king

`te` is again the fourth form of `tumha`, governed by `svāgataṃ`.

:::

---

<a name="m295"></a>

### §295 (307): Apādāne pañcamī (⑤ for the `apādāna` [point of departure]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (liṅgamhā) |    | (hoti)    | pañcamī (vibhatti) | | apādāne (kārake) |
|        | (after the stem) | | (is used) | the fifth form ⑤ | | for the `apādāna` (point of departure) |

`apādāne` is the seventh form of `apādāna`, the kāraka defined at [§271 (88, 308): Yasmā dapeti bhayamādatte vā tadapādānaṃ](#m271) — that from which something departs, from which a fear arises, from which one takes — and widened by §272–§275; its vutti pointed forward to this rule (`Apādānamiccanena kvattho? Apādāne pañcamī`). `pañcamī` ("the fifth") is the fifth of the seven forms of [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55), the pair `smā`, `hi`; the vutti supplies `kārake` and `vibhatti`. There is no option marker: the fifth is the general expression of the `apādāna`. [§296 (314): Kāraṇatthe ca](#m296) extends it to the cause, [§309 (318): Dutiyāpañcamīnañca](#m309) and [§312 (326): Pañcamyatthe ca](#m312) let the sixth and the seventh stand in its sense, and [§248 (260): Kvaci to pañcamyatthe](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m248) gives the suffix `to` the same sense in certain words. After an `a` stem the singular `smā` is commonly `ā` by [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m108) or `mhā` by [§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m99), as the examples show. The Bālāvatāra states the rule at ¶54.

:::

> Apādānakārake pañcamīvibhatti hoti.

> Pāpā cittaṃ nivāraye, abbhā muttova candimā, bhayā muccati so naro.

In the `apādāna` kāraka (the point of departure) the fifth inflection form ⑤ is used.

:::tip[Examples]

> `pāpā cittaṃ nivāraye` \
> [(18Dh 9.1: Cūḷekasāṭakabrāhmaṇavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/9/9.1#125) \
🚻⑤⨀(pāpa) 🚻②⨀(citta) ⏯🤟⨀(nivāreti) \
from evil | the mind | one should hold back

`pāpā` is the `apādāna` in ⑤⨀, that from which the mind is kept away; `cittaṃ` is the `kamma` in ② by [§297 (284): Kammatthe dutiyā](#m297).

> `abbhā muttova candimā` \
> [(10M 4.6: Aṅgulimālasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/4/4.6#1018) \
🚻⑤⨀(abbha) 🚹①⨀(mutta) 🔼(iva) 🚹①⨀(candima) \
from a cloud | freed | like | the moon

`abbhā` is the `apādāna` in ⑤⨀, the cloud from which the moon comes free; `muttova` is `mutto iva`.

> `bhayā muccati so naro` \
🚻⑤⨀(bhaya) ▶️🤟⨀(muccati) 🚹①⨀(ta) 🚹①⨀(nara) \
from danger | is freed | that | man

`bhayā` is the `apādāna` in ⑤⨀ — the fear or danger one gets free of, the second head of the definition in §271 (`bhayaṃ ādatte`). The phrase is not attested in this wording in the Tipiṭaka.

|     | pāpa + smā (⑤⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pāpa + (smā→ā)         | §108 |
| →   | pāp + (~~a~~ + ā)      | §83  |
| →   | **pāpā**               |      |

:::

---

<a name="m296"></a>

### §296 (314): Kāraṇatthe ca (and ⑤ in the sense of cause) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | (liṅgamhā) |    | (hoti)    | (pañcamī vibhatti) | | kāraṇatthe |
| and    | (after the stem) | | (is used) | (the fifth form ⑤) | | in the sense of cause |

`kāraṇatthe` is `kāraṇa-atthe`, "in the sense of a cause"; the Rūpasiddhi (314) glosses `kāraṇa` as "what brings about its own result", the productive cause. `pañcamī` continues from [§295 (307): Apādāne pañcamī](#m295). By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §295: the fifth form marks the cause of a state or event, which is not an `apādāna` by the definition of [§271 (88, 308): Yasmā dapeti bhayamādatte vā tadapādānaṃ](#m271), since nothing departs from it. The cause already has an expression, the third form of [§289 (297): Hetvatthe ca](#m289) (`annena vasati`), and §275 counted `hetvatthe` among the senses given the fifth (`kasmā hetunā`); the Rūpasiddhi accordingly reads this rule as offering an option (`vikappenāyaṃ`) beside that third. The fifth of cause is the form of the `-ā` in `avijjāpaccayā saṅkhārā` and of verbal nouns such as `ananubodhā`.

:::

> Kāraṇatthe ca pañcamīvibhatti hoti.

> Ananubodhā appaṭivedhā catunnaṃ ariyasaccānaṃ yathābhūtaṃ adassanā.

And in the sense of a cause the fifth inflection form ⑤ is used.

:::tip[Example]

> `ananubodhā appaṭivedhā catunnaṃ ariyasaccānaṃ yathābhūtaṃ adassanā` \
> [(3V 6.16: Koṭigāmesaccakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/6/6.16#1309) \
🚹⑤⨀(ananubodha) 🚹⑤⨀(appaṭivedha) ⚧️⑥⨂(catu) 🚻⑥⨂(ariyasacca) 🔼(yathābhūtaṃ) 🚻⑤⨀(adassana) \
through not understanding | through not penetrating | of the four | noble truths | as they really are | through not seeing

`ananubodhā`, `appaṭivedhā` and `adassanā` are the causes, in ⑤⨀ — "because of not understanding, not penetrating, not seeing" — of the long wandering the passage goes on to name; `catunnaṃ ariyasaccānaṃ`, the object of these verbal nouns, takes the sixth form by [§309 (318): Dutiyāpañcamīnañca](#m309). The vutti runs together two passages of the Mahāvagga: the prose `catunnaṃ, bhikkhave, ariyasaccānaṃ ananubodhā appaṭivedhā evamidaṃ dīghamaddhānaṃ sandhāvitaṃ saṃsaritaṃ` and the half-verse that follows it,

> `catunnaṃ ariyasaccānaṃ, yathābhūtaṃ adassanā` \
> [(3V 6.16: Koṭigāmesaccakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/6/6.16#1310) \
⚧️⑥⨂(catu) 🚻⑥⨂(ariyasacca) 🔼(yathābhūtaṃ) 🚻⑤⨀(adassana) \
of the four | noble truths | as they really are | through not seeing

:::

---

<a name="m297"></a>

### §297 (284): Kammatthe dutiyā (② for the `kamma` [object]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (liṅgamhā) |    | (hoti)    | dutiyā (vibhatti) | | kammatthe |
|        | (after the stem) | | (is used) | the second form ② | | in the sense of the `kamma` (object) |

`kammatthe` is `kamma-atthe`, "in the sense of the object": `kamma` is the kāraka defined at [§280 (285): Yaṃ karoti taṃ kammaṃ](#m280) — what one does, sees or hears — whose vutti pointed forward to this rule (`Kammamiccanena kvattho? Kammatthe dutiyā`). `dutiyā` ("the second") is the second of the seven forms of [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55), the pair `aṃ`, `yo`; the vutti supplies `vibhatti`. There is no option marker: the second is the general expression of the object, and it is the topic (`adhikāra`, the Rūpasiddhi says) of the three rules that follow — [§298 (287): Kāladdhānamaccantasaṃyoge](#m298), [§299 (288): Kammappavacanīyayutte](#m299) and [§300 (286): Gati buddhi bhuja paṭha hara kara sayādīnaṃ kārite vā](#m300) — which give the second to words that are not objects by §280.
